\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{C}\!*_{\omega,\delta}\!(d,e)=\big(\mathscr{C}\!*_{\omega}\!d\big)*_{\delta}e
.
By uniqueness of normal form in both HNN-extensions it is clear that the product
v=(d^{e^{0}}d^{e^{1}})^{k}=(d\,e^{-1}d\,e)^{k}
of the
stable
letters
d,e
is in normal form in
\mathscr{A}
. A normal form involving only stable letters is inside
\mathscr{C}

(and in particular, in
F
)
only
if it is trivial.

6.3.
The proof for the operations
\iota
and
\upsilon

Suppose
\mathcal{Y}=\iota(\mathcal{X}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{2})=\mathcal{X}_{1}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2}
, and
the finitely presented overgroups

K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}
and
K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}
of
F
together with finitely generated subgroups

L_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}

and

L_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}

are explicitly given:
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}=\langle\,Z_{1}\;|\;S_{1}\rangle
and
K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}=\langle\,Z_{2}\;|\;S_{2}\rangle
, while the (finitely many) generators of
F
,
L_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}
,
L_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}
can effectively be computed via the generators from
Z_{1}
,
Z_{2}
, respectively.

Since
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}
and
K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}
both are overgroups of
F
, their intersection contains
F
.
Without loss of generality we may assume
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}\!\cap K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}
is strictly
equal
to
F
because these two overgroups are built independently, and the only requirement they share is to contain
F
. I.e., we may assume none of the generators of
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}
, except
a,b,c
, has been used in construction of
K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}
.

Then the
✻
-construction
K_{\mathcal{Y}}=\textstyle{\textrm{\footnotesize✻}}_{i=1}^{2}(K_{\mathcal{X}_{i}},L_{\mathcal{X}_{i}},v_{i})_{F}

build for
G=M=F

is finitely presented, and
by Lemma
5.4
and
Lemma
5.5

we have:

F\cap F^{v_{1}v_{2}}=A_{1}\cap\,A_{2}=\big(F\cap L_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}\big)\cap\big(F\cap L_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}\big)\,=\,A_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}\cap\,A_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}=A_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\cap\,\mathcal{X}_{2}}=\,A_{\mathcal{Y}}.

As a finitely generated subgroup of
K_{\mathcal{Y}}
choose
L_{\mathcal{Y}}=F^{v_{1}v_{2}}
with just
three
generators
a^{v_{1}v_{2}}\!,

b^{v_{1}v_{2}}\!,

c^{v_{1}v_{2}}
. As to explicit presentation of
K_{\mathcal{Y}}
, we may use (
5.2
) and (
5.1
) to write:

(6.2)

\begin{split}K_{\mathcal{Y}}&\!=\!\big\langle\,Z_{1},Z_{2},v_{1},v_{2}\mathrel{\,|\,}a(Z_{1})\!=\!a(Z_{2}),\,b(Z_{1})\!=\!b(Z_{2}),\,c(Z_{1})\!=\!c(Z_{2});\\
&\hskip 99.58464pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes the generators of $L_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}$};\\
&\hskip 99.58464pt\text{$v_{2}$ fixes the generators of $L_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}$}\big\rangle\end{split}

where
a(Z_{1})
is the copy of
a
written as a word on
Z_{1}
,
a(Z_{2})
is the copy of
a
written as a word on
Z_{2}
, etc.;
we made them equal to guarantee
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}\!\cap K_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}=F
.

Notice that in (
6.2
) we did not include any relations identifying two copies of
M

(compare to (
5.1
)) because here
M=F
, and we had already identified the copies of
F
in both groups in the first row of (
6.2
).

Figure 5.
Construction of
K_{\mathcal{Y}}
for
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{X}_{1}\cap\mathcal{X}_{2}
and for
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{X}_{1}\cup\mathcal{X}_{2}
.

The case
\mathcal{Y}=\upsilon(\mathcal{X}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{2})=\mathcal{X}_{1}\cup\mathcal{X}_{2}
is analogous to the proof above, with one difference: we have
A_{\mathcal{Y}}=A_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\cup\,\mathcal{X}_{2}}=\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}_{1}},\;A_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}\rangle
. Using Lemma
5.4
and
Lemma
5.6

we have:

F\cap\langle\,F^{v_{1}}\!,\;F^{v_{2}}\rangle=\langle\,A_{1},\;A_{2}\rangle=\big\langle F\cap L_{\mathcal{X}_{1}},\;F\cap L_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}\big\rangle\,=\,\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}_{1}},\;A_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}\rangle=A_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\cup\,\mathcal{X}_{2}}=\,A_{\mathcal{Y}}.

This time we choose
L_{\mathcal{Y}}=\langle\,F^{v_{1}}\!,\;F^{v_{2}}\rangle
with just
six
generators

a^{v_{1}}\!,

b^{v_{1}}\!,

c^{v_{1}}\!,

a^{v_{2}}\!,

b^{v_{2}}\!,

c^{v_{2}}
.
As
K_{\mathcal{Y}}
we take the same group
\mathscr{A}
used above admitting explicit presentation (
6.2
).

6.4.
Some auxiliary adaptations

Before we proceed to the remaining Higman operations we need some adaptation in notation and construction.

6.4.1.
Adding
a,b,c
to the generators
Z

Assume the hypothesis of Theorem
A
holds for
\mathcal{X}
:
the group
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
with its subgroup
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
are given explicitly, and the embedding
of
F=\langle a,b,c\rangle
into
K_{\mathcal{X}}
is explicitly known.
Since this embedding is explicit, it is possible to write the free generators
a,b,c
as certain words
a=a(Z),\,b=b(Z),\,c=c(Z)
on the alphabet
Z
.
In many cases, such as the proofs in
[
33
]
,
K_{\mathcal{X}}
already is constructed so that
Z
contains the letters
a,b,c
.
But even if
K_{\mathcal{X}}
is given by some other generators
not
involving
a,b,c
, we can apply Tietze transformations: add the words
a=a(Z),\,b=b(Z),\,c=c(Z)
to the defining relations
S
, and add the letters
a,b,c
to the generators
Z
. Hence we can always assume the generators of
F
are included among the letters in
Z
. This is going to simplify the notation below.

6.4.2.
An auxiliary copy
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
of
\mathscr{A}

In analogy to the generating set
X_{\!\mathscr{A}}
given in (
5.11
) introduce a new generating set:

(6.3)

X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}=\big\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c},\bar{t}_{1},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{1},\bar{u}_{1},\bar{u}_{2},\bar{d},\bar{e}\big\}

to construct a copy
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
of the group
\mathscr{A}
applying the same procedure as in Section
5.2
.
This group has the relations
R_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}
obtained from the relations
R_{\!{\mathscr{A}}}
of (
5.10
) by just appending bars on each letter, such as

\bar{b}^{\bar{t}_{1}}\!=\!\bar{b}
,

\bar{b}^{\bar{t}^{\prime}_{1}}\!=\!\bar{b}^{\bar{c}^{\;-1}}
​​​,  etc.
In particular, inside
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
the subgroup

\bar{F}\!=\!\langle\bar{a},\bar{c},\bar{c}\rangle
is a free group of rank
3
.

Next, in addition to the elements
b_{i},b_{f},a_{f}\!\in\!F
,

d_{i},d_{f}\!\in\!{\mathscr{A}}
we introduce the elements

\bar{b}_{i},\bar{b}_{f},\bar{a}_{f}\!\in\!\bar{F}
,

\bar{d}_{i},\bar{d}_{f}\!\in\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}

expectedly defined as
\bar{b}_{i}\!=\bar{b}^{\bar{c}^{i}}
​​,

\bar{b}_{f}\!=\cdots\bar{b}_{-1}^{f(-1)}\bar{b}_{0}^{f(0)}\bar{b}_{1}^{f(1)}\cdots
,

\bar{a}_{f}\!=\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}
;

\bar{d}_{i}=\bar{d}^{\bar{e}^{i}}
​​,

\bar{d}_{f}=\cdots\bar{d}_{-1}^{f(-1)}\bar{d}_{0}^{f(0)}\bar{d}_{1}^{f(1)}\cdots
,
compare to Section
2.4
.

6.4.3.
Construction of the direct product
K_{P}=\bar{\mathcal{K}}\times\mathscr{A}

If for the given
\mathcal{X}\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}
the subgroup
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
F
, then
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\bar{a}_{f}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle
clearly is benign in
\bar{F}
. In case the overgroup
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and its subgroup
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
can explicitly be constructed for
A_{\mathcal{X}}
, the respective
\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,\bar{Z}\;|\;\bar{S}\rangle
and
\bar{L}_{\mathcal{X}}\leq\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}
can explicitly be built for
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Since
\mathscr{A}
was built by adjoining some new letters
t_{1},t^{\prime}_{1},u_{1},u_{2},d,e
to
F=\langle a,b,c\rangle
, we may suppose none of these new letters was involved in construction of
K_{\mathcal{X}}
. Since
K_{\mathcal{X}}
by construction contains
a,b,c
, compare to Point
6.4.1
, we can assume its intersection with
\mathscr{A}
is
F
precisely, and so it is legal to define the group
\mathcal{K}=K_{\mathcal{X}}*_{F}\mathscr{A}
in which:

\mathscr{A}\cap L_{\mathcal{X}}=\big(\mathscr{A}\cap L_{\mathcal{X}}\big)\cap F=\mathscr{A}\cap\big(L_{\mathcal{X}}\cap F\big)=\mathscr{A}\cap A_{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq A_{\mathcal{X}}.

On the other hand,
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is inside both
\mathscr{A}
and
L_{\mathcal{X}}
, and so

\mathscr{A}\cap L_{\mathcal{X}}=A_{\mathcal{X}}
. That is,
A_{\mathcal{X}}
also is benign in the
larger
group
\mathscr{A}
for the finitely presented overgroup
\mathcal{K}
and for the same finitely generated
L_{\mathcal{X}}
mentioned above.

Using the copy
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
of
\mathscr{A}
from Point
6.4.2
, and modifying the steps above for the generators (
6.3
), we get the copies

\bar{F},\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},\bar{\mathcal{K}},\bar{L}_{\mathcal{X}}
of the groups
F,\,A_{\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{K}},L_{\mathcal{X}}
, so that
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
also is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
for

\bar{\mathcal{K}}

and

\bar{L}_{\mathcal{X}}
.

Figure 6.
Construction of
K_{P}=\bar{\mathcal{K}}\times\mathscr{A}
.

By Remark
2.1

the free subgroup

\langle a,d,e\rangle
is benign in
\mathscr{A}

for the finitely presented
\mathscr{A}
and for the finitely generated
\langle a,d,e\rangle
.
Hence, the direct product:

P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times\langle a,d,e\rangle

is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}\times\mathscr{A}
for the finitely presented overgroup

K_{P}=\bar{\mathcal{K}}\times\mathscr{A}
and for the finitely generated subgroup
L_{P}=\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}\!\times\langle a,d,e\rangle\leq K_{P}
.

For each
f\!\in\!\mathcal{X}
we by Lemma
5.12
evidently have
a_{f}\!=a^{b_{f}}\!=a^{d_{f}}
​, this simple fact can be explained for, say,
f=(2,5,3)
:

(6.4)

a^{d_{f}}\!=a^{d_{0}^{2}\,d_{1}^{5}\,d_{2}^{3}}\!=(a^{d_{0}^{2}})^{\,d_{1}^{5}\,d_{2}^{3}}\!=(a^{b_{0}^{2}})^{\,d_{1}^{5}\,d_{2}^{3}}\!=(a^{b_{0}^{2}\,b_{1}^{5}})^{\,d_{2}^{3}}\!=a^{b_{0}^{2}\,b_{1}^{5}\,b_{2}^{3}}\!=a^{b_{f}}\!=a_{f}.

Hence,
A_{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\langle a,d,e\rangle
, and similarly,

\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle=\langle\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle\subseteq\langle\bar{a},\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle
, i.e., the above product
P
certainly is inside
\langle\bar{a},\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle\times\langle a,d,e\rangle
also.
We are going to use this fact in the proofs below often.

6.5.
The proof for the operation
\rho

The case with operation
\rho
was recently covered in
[
34
]
, but we include it in points
6.5.1
–
6.5.3
below to have complete proofs for
all
operations (
H
) here.
Denote
\mathcal{Y}=\rho\mathcal{X}
, say, for
f=(2,5,3)\in\mathcal{X}
the function
\rho f
sends
-2,-1,0
respectively to
3,5,2
, and all other integers
i
to
0
; notice that one
cannot
write
\rho f
as
(3,5,2)
.
Also in analogy with the copy
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
for
\mathscr{A}
in Point
6.4.2
, we may pick a copy
\bar{K}_{{\mathcal{X}}}=\langle\,\bar{Z}\;|\;\bar{S}\rangle
of
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
on some new generators
\bar{Z}
.

6.5.1.
Obtaining the benign subgroup
Q
for
\rho

For each function
f\!\in{\mathcal{E}}
define in
{\mathscr{A}}
the couple of auxiliary products:

\begin{split}d_{\rho f}=\cdots d_{1}^{f(-1)}d_{0}^{f(0)}d_{-1}^{f(1)}\cdots\quad\;{\rm and}\quad\quad\tilde{d}_{\rho f}=\cdots d_{-1}^{f(1)}d_{0}^{f(0)}d_{1}^{f(-1)}\cdots\end{split}

where
\tilde{d}_{\rho f}
differs from
d_{\rho f}
by
reverse order
of its factors
d_{i}^{f(-i)}
only. For example,
for
f=(2,5,3)
we have

d_{\rho f}=d_{-2}^{3}d_{-1}^{5}d_{0}^{2}

and

\tilde{d}_{\rho f}=d_{0}^{2}d_{-1}^{5}d_{-2}^{3}
; compare these with the element
\bar{d}_{f}=\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\bar{d}_{2}^{3}
used above.

In the direct product

\bar{\mathscr{A}}\times{\mathscr{A}}
choose the pairs
\lambda_{f}=\big(\bar{d}_{f},\;\tilde{d}_{\rho f}\!\big)
.
The
3
-generator subgroup

T=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{d},\;d),\;(\bar{e},\;e^{-1})\big\rangle
of this direct product clearly contains such

\lambda_{f}
for every
f\!\in\mathcal{E}
.
This uncomplicated fact requires routine calculations, which are easier to explain on a simple example for, say,
f=(2,5,3)
.
Clearly,
T
contains the product

(\bar{e},\;e^{-1})^{-2}\cdot\,(\bar{d},\;d)\cdot\,(\bar{e},\;e^{-1})^{2}=\big(\bar{d}^{\,\bar{e}^{\;\,2}}\!\!,\;d^{e^{\,-2}}\big)=\big(\bar{d}_{2},\;d_{\,-2}\big),

together with the cube

\big(\bar{d}_{2},\;d_{\,-2}\big)^{3}=\big(\bar{d}_{2}^{3},\;d_{\,-2}^{3}\big)
of the latter.
Similarly, the product

\lambda_{f}=\big(\bar{d}_{0}^{2},\;d_{0}^{2}\big)\cdot\big(\bar{d}_{1}^{5},\;d_{-1}^{5}\big)\cdot\big(\bar{d}_{2}^{3},\;d_{-2}^{3}\big)=\big(\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\bar{d}_{2}^{3},\;\;d_{0}^{2}d_{-1}^{5}d_{-2}^{3}\big)=\big(\bar{d}_{f},\;\tilde{d}_{\rho f}\!\big)

also is in
T
. Trivially,
T
contains the conjugates
(\bar{a},\;a)^{\lambda_{f}}=\big(\bar{a}^{\;\bar{d}_{f}},\;a^{\tilde{d}_{\rho f}}\!\big)
for
all
such
f
.

By Remark
2.1

T

is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}
, and for it one can choose
K_{T}=\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}
and
L_{T}=T
.

Figure 7.
Construction of
K_{Q}
.

Then according to Corollary
5.7
(
1
) the intersection
Q=T\,\cap\,P
of the above two benign subgroups is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}
for the finitely presented overgroup:

(6.5)

\begin{split}K_{Q}&=\big(K_{T}*_{L_{T}}v_{1}\big)*_{\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}}\big(K_{P}*_{L_{P}}v_{2}\big)\\
&=\Big(\big(\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}\big)*_{T}v_{1}\Big)\,*_{\bar{\mathscr{A}}\times{\mathscr{A}}}\Big(\big(\bar{\mathcal{K}}\!\times\!\mathscr{A}\big)*_{\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}\times\,\langle a,d,e\rangle}v_{2}\Big)\end{split}

with two new stable letters
v_{1},v_{2}
, and for the
18
-generator subgroup
L_{Q}\!=\!\big(\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}\big)^{v_{1}v_{2}}
​ of
K_{Q}
.

Q
turns out to have simple structure. Namely, any couple from
Q
is in
P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times\langle a,d,e\rangle
, and hence its first coordinate is an element in
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
generated by some words
\bar{a}_{f}
for certain
f\!\in\mathcal{X}
. For each of them using Lemma
5.12
we in analogy with (
6.4
) have:

(6.6)

\bar{a}_{f}=\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}=\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}\!,

i.e., that first coordinate can be rewritten as a word on
\bar{a},\bar{d},\bar{e}
.
On the other hand, our couple is inside the
3
-generator group
T=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{d},\;d),\;(\bar{e},\;e^{-1})\big\rangle
, i.e., if its first coordinate is written as a word on

\bar{a},\bar{d},\bar{e}
, then the second coordinate can be obtained by replacing all

\bar{a},\bar{d},\bar{e}
in that word by
a,d,e^{-1}
respectively.
But that replacement simply transforms each
\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}
to
a^{\tilde{d}_{\rho f}}
. The routine of this step is very easy to see on an example with, say,
f=(2,5,3)
for which the first coordinate is:

\begin{split}\bar{a}_{f}&=\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}=\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}=\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\bar{d}_{2}^{3}}=\bar{d}_{2}^{\;-3}\bar{d}_{1}^{\;-5}\bar{d}_{0}^{\;-2}\,\cdot\,\bar{a}\,\cdot\,\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\,\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\,\bar{d}_{2}^{3}\\
&=\bar{e}^{\;-2}\,\bar{d}^{\,-3}\,\bar{e}^{\;2}\cdot\bar{e}^{\;-1}\,\bar{d}^{\,-5}\,\bar{e}\cdot\bar{d}^{-2}\,\cdot\;\,\bar{a}\,\;\cdot\;\bar{d}^{2}\cdot\bar{e}^{\;-1}\,\bar{d}^{\,5}\,\bar{e}\cdot\bar{e}^{\;-2}\,\bar{d}^{\,3}\,\bar{e}^{\;2}\end{split}

with respect to which the second coordinate of the couple turns out to be:

\begin{split}&e^{\;2}\,d^{\,-3}\,e^{\;-2}\cdot\,e\,d^{\,-5}\,e^{\;-1}\cdot\,d^{-2}\cdot\,\,a\,\,\cdot\,d^{2}\cdot\,e\,d^{\,5}\,e^{\;-1}\cdot\,e\,d^{\,3}\,e^{\;-2}\\
&=d_{-2}^{\;-3}d_{-1}^{\;-5}d_{0}^{\;-2}\,\cdot\,\,a\,\,\cdot\,d_{0}^{2}\,d_{-1}^{5}\,d_{-2}^{3}=a^{d_{0}^{2}\,d_{-1}^{5}\,d_{-2}^{3}}=a^{\tilde{d}_{\rho f}}.\end{split}

This means that the benign subgroup
Q
of
\bar{\mathscr{A}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{A}}
actually is of a simple format:

Q=\big\langle\big(\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}},\;a^{\tilde{d}_{\rho f}}\!\big)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a)^{\lambda_{f}}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle.

Using this with (
6.6
) we see that
Q
lies inside
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
, and so
Q
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
also for the same choice of
K_{Q}
and
L_{Q}
made earlier.

6.5.2.
“Extracting”
A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
from
Q

Next we have to modify the constructed benign subgroup
Q
by a few steps “to extract” the benign subgroup

A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}=\big\langle a^{b_{\rho f}}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle
from it.

Comparing (
6.4
) to Remark
5.13
we see that
a^{\tilde{d}_{\rho f}}=a^{b_{\rho f}}=a_{\rho f}
, that is,
A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
is nothing but the group generated by the second coordinates
a^{\tilde{d}_{\rho f}}
of all pairs from
Q
.

\bar{F}\cong\bar{F}\times\{1\}
is benign in

\bar{F}\times F
for the finitely presented
\bar{F}\times F
and for the finitely generated
\bar{F}\times\{1\}
, see Remark
2.1
.
Hence, by Corollary
5.7
(
2
) the join

Q_{1}=\big\langle\bar{F}\!\times\!\{1\},\,Q\big\rangle=\bar{F}\!\times\!\langle a_{\rho f}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented overgroup

K_{Q_{1}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\bar{F}\times\{1\}}w_{1}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big(K_{Q}*_{L_{Q}}w_{2}\big)

with two further new stable letters
w_{1},w_{2}
,  and for its
12
-generator subgroup

L_{Q_{1}}=\big\langle(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{1}}\!,\;(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{2}}\big\rangle.

Figure 8.
“Extracting”
Q_{1}
and
A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
from
Q
.

Finally,

F=\{1\}\!\times\!F
is benign in

\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
, and for the finitely generated
\{1\}\!\times\!F
.
Hence,
by Corollary
5.7
(
1
)
the intersection:

A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}=A_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big(\{1\}\!\times\!F\big)\cap Q_{1}=\langle a_{\rho f}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented overgroup:

K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}=K_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\{1\}\times F}w_{3}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}(K_{Q_{1}}*_{L_{Q_{1}}}w_{4})

with stable letters
w_{3},w_{4}
,  and for the
6
-generator subgroup

L_{\rho\mathcal{X}}=L_{\mathcal{Y}}=(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{3}w_{4}}
inside
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
.
But since
A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
is in
F
, it is benign in
F
also, for the same choice of
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
and
L_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
above.

6.5.3.
Writing
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
explicitly

Now from sections
5.2
and
6.4.2
we for
\mathscr{A}
and
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
know their generating sets (
5.11
) and (
6.3
), along with their presentations

\mathscr{A}=\langle\,X_{\!\mathscr{A}}\;|\;R_{\!\mathscr{A}}\rangle

and

\bar{\mathscr{A}}=\langle\,X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}\;|\;R_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}\rangle
.
Also, the groups

K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and
\bar{K}_{{\mathcal{X}}}=\langle\,\bar{Z}\;|\;\bar{S}\rangle
together with the finitely many generators of
L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
and of
\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}

are supposed to be explicitly known.
By Point
6.4.1
we may suppose

X_{\!\mathscr{A}}\cap\,Z=\{a,b,c\}
and

X_{\!\mathscr{\bar{A}}}\cap\,\bar{Z}=\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}
.

The amalgamated product
\mathcal{K}=K_{\mathcal{X}}*_{F}\mathscr{A}

can be generated by the generators

Z\backslash\{a,b,c\}
of
K_{\mathcal{X}}
, together with the generators
X_{\!{\mathscr{A}}}
(we exclude
a,b,c
from
Z
because they already were included in
X_{\!{\mathscr{A}}}
, see Point
6.4.1
).
As defining relations for the group
\mathcal{K}
we may take the union
S\cup R_{\!\mathscr{A}}
. We can similarly treat the copy
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
of
{\mathcal{K}}
.

Then the direct product
K_{P}=\bar{\mathcal{K}}\times\mathscr{A}

can be given by the above mentioned generators and relation,
plus
the relations making sure
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
commutes with
\mathscr{A}
:

(6.7)

\begin{split}K_{P}=\Big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{A}},\;X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}\,;\;\;\bar{Z}\,\backslash\,\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}\;\;\mathrel{|}\;\;R_{\mathscr{\!A}};\;R_{\mathscr{\!\bar{A}}};\;\bar{S};\\
&\hskip-179.25235pt\text{generators $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}$ commute with $X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}$}\,;\;\\
&\hskip-179.25235pt\text{generators $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}$ commute with $\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}$}\Big\rangle.\end{split}

Next, taking into account the way we constructed
K_{T}
,
K_{Q}
,
K_{Q_{1}}
,
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
with the
fixing
effect of our new letters
v_{1},v_{2};\;w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}
on certain finitely generated subgroups, we produce:

(6.8)

\begin{split}K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}=\Big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{A}},\;X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}};\;\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\};\;v_{1},v_{2};\;w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}\;\mathrel{|}\;\;R_{\mathscr{A}};\;R_{\mathscr{\bar{A}}};\;\bar{S};\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{generators $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}$ commute with $X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}$};\;\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{generators $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}$ commute with $\bar{Z}\,\backslash\,\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}$};\;\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes $\bar{a}a,\;\bar{d}d,\;\bar{e}e^{-1}$};\;\;\;\text{$v_{2}$ fixes $\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}$ and $a,d,e$};\;\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$w_{1}$ fixes
$\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}$};\;\;\text{$w_{2}$ fixes $X_{\!\!\mathscr{A}}^{v_{1}v_{2}}$ and $X_{\!\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}^{v_{1}v_{2}}$};\;\;\text{$w_{3}$ fixes $a,b,c$;}\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$w_{4}$ fixes
$\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!w_{1}}\!\cup\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!w_{2}}$}\Big\rangle\,.\end{split}

Lastly, as
L_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
we can take the
6
-generator subgroup
generated by

\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{w_{3}w_{4}}
​ in
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
by Corollary
5.7
(
1
).
In (
6.8
)  “
w_{2}
fixes
X_{\!\!\mathscr{A}}^{v_{1}v_{2}}
and
X_{\!\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}^{v_{1}v_{2}}
” means that conjugation by
w_{2}
fixes
the conjugates of
each
of the generators
X_{\!\!\mathscr{A}}
and
X_{\!\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}

from (
5.11
) or (
6.3
) by the product
v_{1}v_{2}
.

If
K_{\mathcal{X}}
has
m
generators (which we may assume include
a,b,c
) and
n
defining relations, and if

L_{\mathcal{X}}
has
k
generators, then the group
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
in (
6.8
) has

9+9+(m-3)+2+4=m+21
generators and

20+20+n+9\cdot 9+9\cdot(m-3)+3+k+3+3+2\cdot 9+3+2\cdot 6=n+9m+k+136

defining relations.

In the above constructions we have supposed that the overgroup
K_{\mathcal{X}}
of
F
has
a,b,c
among its generators by Point
6.4.1
. Also observe a formatting issue in (
6.8
): we write not
“
v_{1}
fixes

(\bar{a},\;a)
,

(\bar{d},\;d)
,

(\bar{e},\;e^{-1})
”
but
“
v_{1}
fixes

\bar{a}a
,

\bar{d}d
,

\bar{e}e^{-1}
”
which has the same meaning as all generators in

X_{\!\mathscr{A}}
already commute with those in
X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}
according to the second line of (
6.8
).

Remark 6.2
.

Compare the presentation (
6.8
) with Higman’s very brief first paragraph in the proof of Lemma 4.6 on page 470 in
[
15
]
. That paragraph only states that if
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign, then
A_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
is benign because there is an automorphism
\alpha
sending
d_{i}
to
d_{\,-i}
.
Explicit construction of the respective
K_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
and
L_{\rho\mathcal{X}}
is a non-trivial routine task, as we saw above.

Our figures
5
–
8
were to illustrate the Higman operations
\iota,\,\upsilon,\,\rho
. Since the general graphical pattern is understandable, we are
not
going to illustrate the remaining operations
\sigma,\,\tau,\,\theta,\,\zeta,\,\pi,

\omega_{m}

from (
H
).
We will only include more figures in Chapter
7
to illustrate the final stages of the embedding, including the
Higman Rope Trick
in Figure
11
.

6.6.
The proof for the operation
\sigma

Assume
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
F
for the explicitly given finitely presented group
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and for its finitely generated subgroup
L_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Then the copy
\bar{K}_{{\mathcal{X}}}=\langle\,\bar{Z}\;|\;\bar{S}\rangle
of
K_{\mathcal{X}}
can be defined on some new generators
\bar{Z}
.
Denoting
\mathcal{Y}=\sigma\mathcal{X}
we, say, for

f=(2,5,3)\in\mathcal{X}
have

\sigma f=(0,2,5,3)\in\mathcal{Y}
and
a_{\sigma f}=a^{b_{1}^{2}b_{2}^{5}b_{3}^{3}}
.

Remark 6.3
.

For the operation
\sigma
in the currrent section (and for the rest of Higman operations in below sections

6.7
,

6.8
,

6.9
,

6.10
,

6.11
)
we are going to apply constructions considerably distinct from the method used for
\rho
in Section
6.5
above. However, these constructions share some similarities which we wish to stress advisedly. Hence, where reasonable, we will use similar notation, such as

\lambda_{f}
,​

T
,

Q
,

Q_{1}
,​

P
,

\mathcal{K}
, etc., to denote elements and groups with similar purposes in technical steps. Compare, for example, the finitely generated groups
T
used in points
6.5.1
,

6.6.1
,

6.9.3
,

6.10.2
, etc., in very similar tricks.

6.6.1.
Obtaining the benign subgroup
Q
for
\sigma

For each
f\!\in{\mathcal{E}}
we in
F
have:

b_{\sigma f}=\cdots b_{-1+1}^{f(-1)}b_{0+1}^{f(0)}b_{1+1}^{f(1)}\cdots\;\;=\;\;\cdots b_{0}^{f(-1)}b_{1}^{f(0)}b_{2}^{f(1)}\cdots

Say,
for
f=(2,5,3)
we have

b_{\sigma f}=b_{1}^{2}b_{2}^{5}b_{3}^{3}=b_{0}^{0}b_{1}^{2}b_{2}^{5}b_{3}^{3}
.
In

\bar{F}\!\times\!F
define the pairs
\lambda_{f}=\big(\bar{b}_{f},\;b_{\sigma f}\!\big)
for
f\!\in{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then the
3
-generator subgroup

T=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{b},\;b^{c}),\;(\bar{c},\;c)\big\rangle
clearly contains

\lambda_{f}
for every
f\!\in\mathcal{E}
.
Indeed, say, for
f=(2,5,3)
the group
T
contains the conjugate:

(\bar{c},\;c)^{-2}\cdot\,(\bar{b},\;b^{c})\cdot\,(\bar{c},\;c)^{2}=\big(\bar{b}^{\,\bar{c}^{\;\,2}}\!\!,\;b^{c^{1+2}}\big)=\big(\bar{b}_{2},\;b_{3}\big),

together with its cube

\big(\bar{b}_{2}^{3},\;b_{3}^{3}\big)
.
For similar reasons the product

\lambda_{f}=\big(\bar{b}_{0}^{2},\;b_{1}^{2}\big)\cdot\big(\bar{b}_{1}^{5},\;b_{2}^{5}\big)\cdot\big(\bar{b}_{2}^{3},\;b_{3}^{3}\big)=\big(\bar{b}_{0}^{2}\bar{b}_{1}^{5}\bar{b}_{2}^{3},\;\;b_{1}^{2}b_{2}^{5}b_{3}^{3}\big)=\big(\bar{b}_{f},\;b_{\sigma f}\!\big)

also is in
T
. Hence,
T
contains all the conjugates
(\bar{a},\;a)^{\lambda_{f}}=\big(\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}},\;a^{b_{\sigma f}}\!\big)=(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{\sigma f})
,
f\in{\mathcal{E}}
.

Clearly,
T

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
, and for it we can choose
K_{T}=\bar{F}\!\times\!F
and
L_{T}=T
.
Since the subgroup

F
evidently is benign in
F
, the direct product:

P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times F

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented overgroup

K_{P}=\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}\times F
and for the finitely generated subgroup
L_{P}=\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}\!\times F\;\leq\;K_{P}
.

By Corollary
5.7
(
1
) the intersection
Q=T\,\cap\,P
of the above benign subgroups
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented overgroup:

(6.9)

\begin{split}K_{Q}&=\big(K_{T}*_{L_{T}}v_{1}\big)*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big(K_{P}*_{L_{P}}v_{2}\big)\\
&=\Big(\big(\bar{F}\!\times\!F\big)*_{T}v_{1}\Big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}\Big(\big(\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}\!\times\!F\big)*_{\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}\times\,F}v_{2}\Big)\end{split}

with two new stable letters
v_{1},v_{2}
, and for the
6
-generator subgroup
L_{Q}\!=\big(\bar{F}\!\times\!F\big)^{v_{1}v_{2}}
​.

Let us reveal the simple structure of
Q
. Namely, any couple from
Q
is in
P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times F
, and hence, its first coordinate is an element in
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
generated by some
\bar{a}_{f}=\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}
for certain
f\!\in\mathcal{X}
. I.e., that first coordinate can be written as a word on
\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
.
On the other hand, our couple is in
T=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{b},\;b^{c}),\;(\bar{c},\;c)\big\rangle
, i.e., if its first coordinate is a word on

\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
, then the second coordinate can be obtained by replacing in that word each of

\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
by
a,b^{c}\!,\,c
respectively.
But that just transforms
\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}
to
a^{b_{\sigma f}}
because, say, for
f=(2,5,3)
the first coordinate is:

\begin{split}\bar{a}_{f}&=\bar{c}^{\;-2}\,\bar{b}^{\,-3}\,\bar{c}^{\;2}\cdot\bar{c}^{\;-1}\,\bar{b}^{\,-5}\,\bar{c}\cdot\bar{b}^{-2}\,\cdot\;\,\bar{a}\,\;\cdot\;\bar{b}^{2}\cdot\bar{c}^{\;-1}\,\bar{b}^{\,5}\,\bar{c}\cdot\bar{c}^{\;-2}\,\bar{b}^{\,3}\,\bar{c}^{\;2},\end{split}

and the respective second coordinate then has to be:

\begin{split}&c^{-2}\,(b^{c})^{\,-3}\,c^{2}\cdot\,c^{-1}\,(b^{c})^{\,-5}\,c\cdot\,(b^{c})^{-2}\cdot\,\,a\,\cdot\,(b^{c})^{\,2}\cdot\,c^{-1}\,(b^{c})^{\,5}\,c\cdot\,c^{-2}\,(b^{c})^{\,3}\,c\\
&=b_{2+1}^{\;-3}b_{1+1}^{\;-5}b_{0+1}^{\;-2}\,\cdot\,\,a\,\cdot\,b_{0+1}^{2}\,b_{1+1}^{5}\,b_{2+1}^{3}=a^{b_{0+1}^{2}b_{1+1}^{5}b_{2+1}^{3}}=a^{b_{1}^{2}b_{2}^{5}b_{3}^{3}}=a^{b_{\,\sigma f}}\!.\end{split}

Thus the benign subgroup
Q
of
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
, in fact, is:

Q=\big\langle\big(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{\sigma f}\!\big)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle=\big\langle\big(\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}},\;a^{b_{\sigma f}}\!\big)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a)^{\lambda_{f}}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle.

6.6.2.
“Extracting”
A_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
from
Q

Our next objective is to modify the obtained benign subgroup
Q
via a few steps “to extract” the benign subgroup

A_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}=\big\langle a_{\sigma f}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle
from it.

From the previous section we see that
A_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
is nothing but the group generated by the second coordinates
a^{b_{\sigma f}}=a_{\sigma f}
of pairs from
Q
.

\bar{F}\cong\bar{F}\times\{1\}
is benign in

\bar{F}\times F
for the finitely presented
\bar{F}\times F
and finitely generated
\bar{F}\times\{1\}
, see Remark
2.1
.
Hence, by Corollary
5.7
(
2
) the join

Q_{1}=\big\langle\bar{F}\!\times\!\{1\},\,Q\big\rangle=\bar{F}\!\times\!\langle a_{\sigma f}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented:

K_{Q_{1}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\bar{F}\times\{1\}}w_{1}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big(K_{Q}*_{L_{Q}}w_{2}\big)

with two further new letters
w_{1},w_{2}
,  and for its
12
-generator subgroup:

L_{Q_{1}}=\big\langle(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{1}}\!,\;(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{2}}\big\rangle.

F=\{1\}\!\times\!F
is benign in

\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
and finitely generated
\{1\}\!\times\!F
.
Hence,
by Corollary
5.7
(
1
)
the intersection:

A_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}=A_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big(\{1\}\!\times\!F\big)\cap Q_{1}=\langle a_{\sigma f}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented group:

K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}=K_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\{1\}\times F}w_{3}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}(K_{Q_{1}}*_{L_{Q_{1}}}w_{4})

with stable letters
w_{3},w_{4}
,  and for the
6
-generator subgroup

L_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}=L_{\mathcal{Y}}=(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{3}w_{4}}
.
But since
A_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
is inside
F
, it is benign in
F
also, for the same choice of
K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
and
L_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
above.

6.6.3.
Writing
K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
explicitly

Recall that the groups

K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and
\bar{K}_{{\mathcal{X}}}=\langle\,\bar{Z}\;|\;\bar{S}\rangle
together with the finitely many generators of
L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
and of
\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}

are explicitly given.
By Point
6.4.1
we may suppose
Z
includes

a,b,c
, and
\bar{Z}
includes

\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
.

The finitely presented overgroup

K_{P}=\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}\times F

of
P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times F

is given by the relations of
\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}
,
plus
the relations making sure
\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}
commutes with
F
:

(6.10)

\begin{split}K_{P}=\Big\langle a,b,c;\;\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\,;\;\;\bar{Z}\,\backslash\,\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}\;\;\mathrel{|}\;\;\bar{S};\\
&\hskip-147.95433pt\text{$a,b,c$ commute with $\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}$ and $\bar{Z}\,\backslash\,\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}$}\Big\rangle\end{split}

(in the first and second lines we exclude
\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
from
\bar{Z}
because of Point
6.4.1
).

Taking into account the way we constructed
K_{T}
,
K_{Q}
,
K_{Q_{1}}
,
K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
with
fixing
effect of the new letters
v_{1},v_{2};\;w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}
(on respective finitely generated subgroups) we have:

(6.11)

\begin{split}K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}=\Big\langle a,b,c;\;\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c};\;\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\};\;v_{1},v_{2};\;w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4}\;\;\mathrel{|}\;\;\bar{S};\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$a,b,c$ commute with $\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}$ and with $\bar{Z}\,\backslash\,\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}$}\,;\;\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes $\bar{a}a,\;\bar{b}b^{c},\;\bar{c}c$};\;\;\;\text{$v_{2}$ fixes $\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}$ and $a,b,c$};\;\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$w_{1}$ fixes
$\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}$};\;\text{$w_{2}$ fixes $\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!v_{1}v_{2}}$}\!;\;\text{$w_{3}$ fixes $a,b,c$;}\\
&\hskip-281.68228pt\text{$w_{4}$ fixes
$\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!w_{1}}\!\cup\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!w_{2}}$}\Big\rangle\,.\end{split}

As
L_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
we can explicitly take the
6
-generator subgroup

\big\langle a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\rangle^{w_{3}w_{4}}
​ in
K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
by Corollary
5.7
(
1
).
In (
6.11
)  “
w_{2}
fixes
\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!v_{1}v_{2}}
” means that conjugation by
w_{2}
fixes
the conjugates of
each
of the generators
a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
by the product
v_{1}v_{2}
.

If
K_{\mathcal{X}}
has
m
generators (which we may assume include
a,b,c
, see Point
6.4.1
) and
n
defining relations, and if

L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
has
k
generators, then the group
K_{\sigma\mathcal{X}}
in (
6.11
) has

3+3+(m-3)+2+4=m+9
generators and

n+3\cdot 3+3\cdot(m-3)+3+k+3+3+6+3+2\cdot 6=n+3m+k+30

defining relations.

6.7.
The proof for the operation
\zeta

Assume
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
F
for the finitely presented group
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and for the finitely generated
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
. Denote
\mathcal{Y}=\zeta\mathcal{X}
. If, say,

f=(2,5,3)\in\mathcal{X}
, then
\mathcal{Y}
contains all possible triples
f=(n,5,3)
with
n\in{\mdmathbb Z}
, and

A_{\mathcal{Y}}
contains all the elements
a^{b_{0}^{n}b_{1}^{5}b_{2}^{3}}
.

6.7.1.
Construction of
\mathcal{K}
and
K_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}

For the group
\mathscr{A}
from Section
5.2

we can use the argument in Point
6.4.3
, to suppose
K_{\mathcal{X}}\cap\mathscr{A}=F
, and to define

\mathcal{K}=K_{\mathcal{X}}*_{F}\mathscr{A}
.
For any
f\!\in\mathcal{X}
and for any
n\in{\mdmathbb Z}
we by Lemma
5.12
have in
\mathscr{A}
:

(6.12)

a_{f}^{d^{n}}=\big(a_{f}^{d_{0}}\big)^{d_{0}^{n-1}}=a_{f_{0}^{+}}^{d_{0}^{n-1}}=\cdots=a_{g}

where
g(0)=f(0)+n
, and
g(i)=f(i)
for any
i\neq 0
. Here we assumed
n
to be positive, but the negative case is covered using
f_{0}^{-}
, and so we have
A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle
.
Since
A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}\subseteq F
, then also

A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}\subseteq F\cap\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle
.

To show the reverse inclusion apply the “conjugates collecting” process (
2.2
)
for the sets
{\mathfrak{X}}=\big\{a_{f}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\}

and
{\mathfrak{Y}}=\{d\}
.
We can write every word
w\in\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle=\langle{\mathfrak{X}},{\mathfrak{Y}}\rangle
as a product of words
u,v
via:

(6.13)

w=u\cdot v=a_{f_{1}}^{\pm v_{1}}a_{f_{2}}^{\pm v_{2}}\cdots a_{f_{k}}^{\pm v_{k}}\cdot v

where all
f_{i}
are in
\mathcal{X}
(and hence, all
a_{f_{i}}
are in
{\mathfrak{X}}
), and the words
v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{k},\;v\in\langle{\mathfrak{Y}}\rangle

simply are some powers of
d
.
As we saw above
a_{f_{i}}^{\pm v_{i}}=a_{f_{i}}^{\pm d^{n_{i}}}=a_{g_{i}}\in A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}
, i.e.,
u
always belongs to
A_{\mathcal{Y}}=A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}

in (
6.13
), and so
u\in F
.
Thus, whenever
w\in F
, then also
v\in F
holds. But from the last step of construction of
\mathscr{A}
(as an HNN-extension) in Section
5.2
it is evident that a power of the stable letter
d
is in
F
only if it is trivial, and so

A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}=F\cap\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle
.

A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign not only in
F
but also in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented
\mathcal{K}
and for the same finitely generated
L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
mentioned at the beginning of this section.
\langle d\rangle
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
by Remark
2.1
. Hence,
by Corollary
5.7
(
2
) the join
\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented group:

K_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle}=\big(\mathcal{K}*_{L_{\mathcal{X}}}v_{1}\big)\,*_{\mathcal{K}}\big(\mathcal{K}*_{\langle d\rangle}v_{2}\big),

and for its finitely generated subgroup
L_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle}=\langle\,\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{1}},\;\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{2}}\rangle
.

As
F
also is benign in
\mathcal{K}
, by Corollary
5.7
(
1
) the intersection
A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented overgroup:

K_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}=K_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big(K_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle}*_{L_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X},\;d}\rangle}}\!v_{3}\big)\,*_{\mathcal{K}}\big(\mathcal{K}*_{F}v_{4}\big),

and for its finitely generated subgroup

L_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}\!=L_{\mathcal{Y}}\!=\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{3}v_{4}}
.
Since
A_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}
entirely is inside
F
, then it also is benign in
F
for the same choice of
K_{\mathcal{Y}}
and
L_{\mathcal{Y}}
above.

6.7.2.
Writing
K_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}
explicitly

Now we can write:

(6.14)

\begin{split}K_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}=\Big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{A}};\;Z\backslash\{a,b,c\};\;v_{1},\ldots,v_{4}\;\mathrel{|}\;\;R_{\mathscr{A}};\;S;\\
&\hskip-162.1807pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes the generators of $L_{\!\mathcal{X}}$};\;\;\;\text{$v_{2}$ fixes $d$};\;\\
&\hskip-162.1807pt\text{$v_{3}$ fixes $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{v_{1}}$, $Z^{v_{1}}$, $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{v_{2}}$, $Z^{v_{2}}$\,;}\;\;\;\text{$v_{4}$ fixes $a,b,c$}\Big\rangle\,.\end{split}

In the first line we exclude
a,b,c
from the generators
Z
by Point
6.4.1
.
As a finitely generated subgroup in
K_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}
take
L_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}=\big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{v_{3}v_{4}}\cup Z^{v_{3}v_{4}}\big\rangle
.

If
K_{\mathcal{X}}
has
m
generators (which we may assume include
a,b,c
, see Point
6.4.1
) and
n
defining relations, and if

L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
has
k
generators, then the group
K_{\zeta\mathcal{X}}
in (
6.14
)
has

9+(m-3)+4=m+10
generators and

20+n+k+1+9+m+9+m+3=2m+n+k+42
relations.

6.8.
The proof for the operation
\pi

Assume
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
F
for the finitely presented group
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and for the finitely generated
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
. Denote
\mathcal{Y}=\pi\mathcal{X}
. If, say,

f=(2,5,3)\in\mathcal{X}
, then
\mathcal{Y}
contains all possible tuples of type
f=(2,j_{1},\ldots,j_{m-1})
for all
m=1,2,\ldots

For this case we are going to mix some constructions from sections
6.7
and
5.1
.

6.8.1.
Construction of a new
\mathcal{K}
and
K_{\pi\mathcal{X}}

In
\mathscr{A}
the stable letters
d,e
clearly generate a free subgroup
\langle d,e\rangle
of rank
2
.
In analogy to the isomorphisms
\xi_{m},\xi^{\prime}_{m}
on
\langle b,c\rangle
in Section
5.1
,
we can define
isomorphisms

{}_{m},{}_{m}^{\prime}
by the rules:

{}_{m}(d)=d_{\,-m+1},\;{}_{m}^{\prime}(d)=d_{\,-m}
,

{}_{m}(e)={}_{m}^{\prime}(e)=e^{2}
​.
Taking
m=1
we have

{}_{1}(d)=d_{0}=d,\;{}_{1}^{\prime}(d)=d_{\,-1}
,

{}_{1}(e)={}_{1}^{\prime}(e)=e^{2}
.
Use these
{}_{1},{}_{1}^{\prime}
to construct the HNN-extension

\mathscr{A}^{*}=\mathscr{A}*_{{}_{1},{}_{1}^{\prime}}(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1})
in which
d,e,x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}
evidently generate the subgroup:

{}_{1}=\langle d,e,x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle=\langle d,e\rangle*_{{}_{1},{}_{1}^{\prime}}(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1})\;\cong\;\Xi_{1}.

Since the analogs of statements on
\Xi_{m}
from Section
5.1
also hold for
m
, we adapting Lemma
5.10
, and denoting
D=\langle d_{1},d_{2},\ldots\,\rangle
have:

(6.15)

\langle d,e\rangle\cap\langle d_{1},x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle=D\quad\text{and also}\quad\mathscr{A}\cap\langle d_{1},x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle=D,

i.e.,
D
is benign in
\mathscr{A}
for the finitely presented overgroup

K_{D}=\mathscr{A}^{*}
and for the finitely generated

L_{D}=\langle d_{1},x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle
.

As in Section
6.7
, we may suppose none of
t_{1},t^{\prime}_{1},u_{1},u_{2},d,e
and
also
none of
x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}
is involved in
K_{\mathcal{X}}
, so

K_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\mathscr{A}^{*}
intersect in
F
, and we can define
\mathcal{K}=K_{\mathcal{X}}*_{F}\mathscr{A}^{*}
(the group
\mathcal{K}
of Section
6.7.1
is the analog of the current
\mathcal{K}
, it plays a similar role, and we prefer to denote them by the same symbol).
For any
f\in\mathcal{X}
, for any
n\in{\mdmathbb Z}
, and for any positive index
j=1,2,\ldots
we, using Lemma
5.12
in analogy with (
6.12
), compute in
\mathscr{A}^{*}
:

(6.16)

a_{f}^{d_{j}^{n}}=\big(a_{f}^{d_{j}}\big)^{d_{j}^{n-1}}=a_{f_{j}^{+}}^{d_{j}^{n-1}}=\cdots=a_{g}

where
g(j)=f(j)+n
, and
g(i)=f(i)
for all
i\neq j
. Here we applied a positive
n
, while the negative case could be covered using
f_{j}^{-}
instead.
Then taking another positive value for
j
we could repeatedly apply the analog of (
6.16
) on
a_{g}
. This way we could after finitely many steps construct arbitrary element from

A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
. Since each
a_{g}
also is in
F
, we arrive to the inclusion

A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}\subseteq F\cap\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},D\rangle
.

To achieve the reverse inclusion apply the “conjugates collecting” process (
2.2
)
for the sets
{\mathfrak{X}}=\big\{a_{f}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\}

and
{\mathfrak{Y}}=\big\{d_{1},d_{2},\ldots\,\big\}
.
Write every word
w\in\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},D\rangle=\langle{\mathfrak{X}},{\mathfrak{Y}}\rangle
as:

(6.17)

w=u\cdot v=a_{f_{1}}^{\pm v_{1}}a_{f_{2}}^{\pm v_{2}}\cdots a_{f_{k}}^{\pm v_{k}}\cdot v

where all
f_{i}
are in
\mathcal{X}
(and hence, all
a_{f_{i}}
are in
{\mathfrak{X}}
), and the
v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{k},\;v\in\langle{\mathfrak{Y}}\rangle

are some words on
d_{1},d_{2},\ldots
, i.e., some words on the letters
d,e
.
As we have shown by repeated application of (
6.16
) above, each
a_{f_{i}}^{\pm v_{i}}
is in
A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
. So
u
also is in
A_{\mathcal{Y}}=A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
and, hence, is in
u\in F
.
Thus, whenever
w\in F
, then
v\in F
also. But from construction of
\mathscr{A}

(as an HNN-extension) in Section
5.2

it is clear that a word on
d_{1},d_{2},\ldots
(i.e., a word on stable letters
d,e
) is in
F
only if it is trivial, and so we have the equality

A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}=F\cap\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},D\rangle
.

D
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
because it is benign in its subgroups
\mathscr{A}
(or even in
\langle d,e\rangle
), see (
6.15
).

A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign not only in
F
but also in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented
\mathcal{K}
and for the same finitely generated
L_{\mathcal{X}}
supposed above.
Hence,
by Corollary
5.7
(
2
) the join
\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},D\rangle
also is benign in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented group:

K_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},d\rangle}=\big(\mathcal{K}*_{L_{\mathcal{X}}}v_{1}\big)*_{\mathcal{K}}\big(\mathcal{K}*_{\langle d_{1},x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\rangle}v_{2}\big),

and for its finitely generated subgroup
L_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},\,D\rangle}=\langle\,\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{1}},\;\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{2}}\rangle
.

As
F
also is benign in
\mathcal{K}
, by Corollary
5.7
(
1
) the intersection
A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}=F\cap\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},D\rangle
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented overgroup:

K_{\pi\mathcal{X}}=K_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big(K_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},\,D\rangle}*_{L_{\langle\,A_{\mathcal{X}},\;D\rangle}}\!v_{3}\big)*_{\mathcal{K}}\big(\mathcal{K}*_{F}v_{4}\big)

and for its finitely generated subgroup

L_{\pi\mathcal{X}}\!=L_{\mathcal{Y}}\!=\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{3}v_{4}}
.
Since
A_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
entirely is inside
F
, then it also is benign in
F
for the same choice of
K_{\mathcal{Y}}
and
L_{\mathcal{Y}}
made above.

6.8.2.
Writing
K_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
explicitly

It remains to explicitly write:

(6.18)

\begin{split}K_{\pi\mathcal{X}}=\Big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{A}};\;Z\backslash\{a,b,c\};\;x_{1},x_{2};\;v_{1},\ldots,v_{4}\;\mathrel{|}\;\;R_{\mathscr{A}};\;S;\\
&\hskip-219.08621pt\text{$x_{1}$ sends $d,e$ to $d,e^{2}$};\;\;\;\;\text{$x^{\prime}_{1}$ sends $d,e$ to $d^{e^{-1}},e^{2}$};\;\\
&\hskip-219.08621pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes the generators of $L_{\!\mathcal{X}}$};\;\;\;\text{$v_{2}$ fixes $d^{e},x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}$};\;\\
&\hskip-219.08621pt\text{$v_{3}$ fixes $X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{\!v_{1}}$, $X^{\!v_{1}}$,
$\{x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\}^{\!v_{1}}$,\;
$X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{\!v_{2}}$, $X^{\!v_{2}}$,
$\{x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\}^{\!v_{2}}$
;}\\
&\hskip-219.08621pt\text{$v_{4}$ fixes $a,b,c$}\Big\rangle\,.\end{split}

We exclude
a,b,c
from the generators
Z
because they already are included in
X_{\!\mathscr{A}}
.
As a finitely generated subgroup in
K_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
take
L_{\pi\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{K}^{v_{3}v_{4}}=\big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{v_{3}v_{4}}\cup Z^{v_{3}v_{4}}\cup\{x_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}\}^{v_{3}v_{4}}\big\rangle
.

If
K_{\mathcal{X}}
has
m
generators (which we may assume include
a,b,c
, see Point
6.4.1
) and
n
defining relations, and if

L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
has
k
generators, then the group
K_{\pi\mathcal{X}}
in (
6.18
)
has

9+(m-3)+2+4=m+12
generators and

20+n+2+2+k+3+2\cdot(9+m+2)+3=n+k+2m+52
defining relations.

6.9.
The proof for the operation
\theta

Assume
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
F
for the explicitly given finitely presented group
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and for the finitely generated
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Denote
\mathcal{Y}=\theta\mathcal{X}
.
If, say,

f=(2,5,3)
or
f=(2,5,3,8)
are in
\mathcal{X}
, then
\mathcal{Y}
contains the couple
\theta f=(2,3)
.

We are free to use the copy
\bar{F}=\langle\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\rangle
of
F
, and suppose the copies

\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,\bar{Z}\;|\;\bar{S}\rangle
and

\bar{L}_{\mathcal{X}}
are given for the copy
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
of
A_{\mathcal{X}}
, see Point
6.4.2
.

6.9.1.
Finding the benign subgroup
O

We reuse the copy

\bar{\mathscr{A}}
of
\mathscr{A}

with the generating set
X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}
from (
6.3
).
In analogy with Point
6.8.1
, in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
the letters
\bar{d},\bar{e}
generate a free subgroup
\langle\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle
, and we can define an isomorphism
\varepsilon
on it sending
\bar{d},\bar{e}
to
\bar{d}_{2},\bar{e}
.
Using it construct the HNN-extension

\bar{\mathscr{A}}^{*}=\bar{\mathscr{A}}*_{\varepsilon}y
inside which
\bar{d},\bar{e},y
clearly generate the HNN-extension:

\langle\bar{d},\bar{e},y\rangle=\langle\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle*_{\varepsilon}y.

Set
O=\langle\bar{d}_{j}\;|\;j\!=\!2k+\!1,\;k\in{\mdmathbb Z}\rangle
to be the subgroup generated by
\bar{d}_{j}
for all odd
j
, and show that
O
is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
. It is easy to show that:

(6.19)

\langle\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle\cap\langle\bar{d}_{1},y\rangle=O\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{\mathscr{A}}\cap\langle\bar{d}_{1},y\rangle=O.

Clearly, only the first of equalities (
6.19
) needs a verification.
For any
j=2k\!+\!1
we have

\bar{d}_{j}=\bar{d}^{\bar{e}^{\;2k+1}}=\bar{d}^{y^{k}\cdot\,\bar{e}}=\bar{d}_{1}^{y^{k}}
​​​,  and so
O\subseteq\langle\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle\cap\langle\bar{d}_{1},y\rangle
.
On the other hand, using the process (
2.4
) applied to
x=\bar{d}_{1}
and to the current
y
we can rewrite any
w\in\langle\bar{d}_{1},y\rangle
as:

(6.20)

w=\bar{d}_{1}^{\;\pm y^{k_{1}}}\bar{d}_{1}^{\;\pm y^{k_{2}}}\!\!\cdots\,\bar{d}_{1}^{\;\pm y^{k_{s}}}\!\!\!\cdot y^{l}=\!u\cdot v\,.

As we just saw, all the
\bar{d}_{1}^{\;\pm y^{k_{i}}}
​​ are equal to some
\bar{d}_{j}
with
odd

j
and, thus,
u\in O
. If also
w\in\langle\bar{d},\bar{e}\rangle
, then
v\!=\!y^{l}
must be trivial, as it is a power of the stable letter
y
of our HNN-extension.
(
6.19
) is proven, and so
O
is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}
for the finitely presented overgroup

K_{O}=\bar{\mathscr{A}}^{*}
and for the finitely generated

L_{O}=\langle\bar{d}_{1},y\rangle
.

6.9.2.
Finding the benign intersection
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}

Denote by
\vartheta\mathcal{X}
the auxiliary set of all functions
f\!\in{\mathcal{E}}
for which there is a
g\in\mathcal{X}
such that
f(j)=g(j)
on all
even
indices
j=2k
, and the value
f(j)
is arbitrary for all
odd
indices
j=2k\!+\!1
. In this step we show that
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
\bar{F}
in order to use this for the required set
\theta\mathcal{X}
later.

We may suppose none of the letters
\bar{t}_{1},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{1},\bar{u}_{1},\bar{u}_{2},\bar{d},\,\bar{e}
and
y
was involved in construction of
\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}
, i.e.,

\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}\cap\bar{\mathscr{A}}^{*}=\bar{F}
, and we can set
\mathcal{K}=\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}*_{\bar{F}}K_{O}=\bar{K}_{\mathcal{X}}*_{\bar{F}}\bar{\mathscr{A}}^{*}

(the current group
\mathcal{K}
of course is different from
\mathcal{K}
used in points
6.7.1
and
6.8.1
, but it plays a similar role):

\mathcal{K}=\Big\langle X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}};\;\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\};\;y;\;a,b,c\;\mathrel{|}\;R_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}};\;\bar{S};\;\text{$y$ sends $\bar{d},\bar{e}$ \,to\, $\bar{d}_{2},\bar{e}$}\Big\rangle.

For below references denote the set of
9+m-3+1+3=m+10
generators of
\mathcal{K}
by:

(6.21)

X_{\!\mathcal{K}}=X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}\cup\,\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}\,\cup\{y;\;a,b,c\}.

For any
f\!\in\mathcal{X}
,
for any positive
n
,
and for any
odd
integer index
j=2k\!+\!1
we again by Lemma
5.12

compute in
\bar{\mathscr{A}}^{*}
:

(6.22)

\bar{a}_{f}^{{\bar{d}}_{j}^{\,n}}=\big(\bar{a}_{f}^{{\bar{d}}_{2k+1}}\big)^{{\bar{d}}_{2k+1}^{\;n\,-1}}=\bar{a}_{f_{2k+1}^{+}}^{\;{\bar{d}}_{2k+1}^{\;n\,-1}}=\cdots=\bar{a}_{g}

where
g(j)=f(j)+n
, and
g(i)=f(i)
for all
i\neq j
. Here we used a positive
n
, while the negative case could be covered by
f_{j}^{-}
.
Choosing yet another
odd

j
we could apply the analog of (
6.22
) on
\bar{a}_{g}
. After finitely many such steps we construct all the elements
\bar{a}_{g}
from

\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
. Since these
\bar{a}_{g}
also are in
\bar{F}
, we arrive to the inclusion

\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\bar{F}\cap\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},O\rangle
.

For the reverse inclusion apply (
2.2
)
for the sets
{\mathfrak{X}}=\big\{\bar{a}_{f}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\}

and
{\mathfrak{Y}}=\big\{\bar{d}_{j}\;|\;j=2k\!+\!1,\;k\in{\mdmathbb Z}\big\}
.
Write every word
w\in\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},O\rangle=\langle{\mathfrak{X}},{\mathfrak{Y}}\rangle
as:

(6.23)

w=u\cdot v=\bar{a}_{f_{1}}^{\;\pm v_{1}}\bar{a}_{f_{2}}^{\;\pm v_{2}}\cdots\bar{a}_{f_{k}}^{\;\pm v_{k}}\cdot v

where all
f_{i}
are in
\mathcal{X}
, and
v_{1},v_{2},\ldots,v_{k},\;v\in\langle{\mathfrak{Y}}\rangle

are words on some letters
\bar{d}_{j}
with various
odd
indices
j
.
As we have seen in (
6.22
), each
\bar{a}_{f_{i}}^{\;\pm v_{i}}
is in
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
. So
u
also is in
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\bar{F}
.
Thus, whenever
w\in\bar{F}
, then
v\in\bar{F}
also. But
v
is a word on stable letters
\bar{d},\bar{e}
and it can be in
\bar{F}
only if it is trivial.

We proved that

\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}=\bar{F}\cap\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},O\rangle
, i.e.,
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
is constructed from three benign subgroups
\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},\,O,\,\bar{F}
by a join and an intersection.
By Corollary
5.7
(
2
) the join
\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},O\rangle
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented group:

K_{\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},\,O\rangle}=\big(\mathcal{K}*_{\bar{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}v_{1}\big)*_{\mathcal{K}}\big(\mathcal{K}*_{\langle\bar{d}_{1},\,y\rangle}v_{2}\big),

and for its finitely generated subgroup
L_{\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},O\rangle}=\langle\,\mathcal{K}^{v_{1}},\;\mathcal{K}^{v_{2}}\rangle
.
Then by Corollary
5.7
(
1
) the intersection
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
\mathcal{K}
for the finitely presented:

K_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}=\big(K_{\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},O\rangle}*_{L_{\langle\,\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}},\;O\rangle}}\!v_{3}\big)*_{\mathcal{K}}\big(\mathcal{K}*_{\bar{F}}v_{4}\big)

and for its finitely generated subgroup

L_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\!=\mathcal{K}^{\,v_{3}v_{4}}
.
Since
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
is in
\bar{F}
, then it also is benign in
\bar{F}
for the same choice of
K_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
and
L_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
just made above.

6.9.3.
Obtaining the benign subgroup
Q
for
\theta

By our construction

\vartheta\mathcal{X}
is the set of all functions
f\!\in{\mathcal{E}}
which coincide with some
g\!\in\mathcal{X}
on all
even
indices, and which may have arbitrary coordinates on
odd
indices.
In particular,
\vartheta\mathcal{X}
contains all those
f\!\in{\mathcal{E}}
which coincide with some
g\!\in\mathcal{X}
on all even indices, and are
zero
on all odd indices.

If

T=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{b},\;b),\;(\bar{c}^{\;2}\!,\;c)\big\rangle
, and

Q=T\cap(\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\!\times F)
, then the combinatorial meaning of this intersection is uncomplicated to understand.
If, say,
f\!=(2,5,3,8)
is in
\mathcal{X}
, then
\vartheta\mathcal{X}
contains
all
functions of type
(2,x,3,y)
with
x,y\in{\mdmathbb Z}
and, in particular, it contains the function
f_{0}
obtained from
f
by changing to zero all its coordinates for odd indices, in this case
f_{0}=(2,0,3,0)
.
Since
\theta
for any
f
simply ignores all coordinates of
f
for odd indices, then

\theta(\vartheta\mathcal{X})=\theta\mathcal{X}
.
In particular, this means that
\theta f_{0}=\theta f
, such as
\theta(2,0,3,0)=\theta(2,5,3,8)=(2,3)
.
Since
T
for any
i
contains the couple
(\bar{b}_{2\,\cdot\,i},\,b_{i})=(\bar{b}^{(\bar{c}^{2})^{i}}\!\!\!,\;\;b^{c^{i}})=(\bar{b},b)^{(\bar{c}^{\;2}\!\!,\;c)^{\,i}}
​​,  it also contains the couples of type
(\bar{a}_{f_{0}},\;a_{\theta f_{0}})
, such as:

(\bar{a},\,a)^{\;(\bar{b}_{2\,\cdot\,0},\;b_{0})^{\,2}\,\cdot\;(\bar{b}_{2\,\cdot\,1},\;b_{1})^{\,3}}=\big(\bar{a}^{\;\bar{b}_{0}^{2}\,\bar{b}_{2}^{3}\,},\;\;a^{\,b_{0}^{2}\,b_{1}^{3}\,}\big)=\big(\bar{a}^{\;\bar{b}_{0}^{2}\;\boldsymbol{\bar{b}_{1}^{0}}\,\bar{b}_{2}^{3}\;\boldsymbol{\bar{b}_{3}^{0}}},\;\;a^{\,b_{0}^{2}\,b_{1}^{3}\,}\big)=(\bar{a}_{f_{0}},\;a_{\theta f_{0}})

(we added the trivial factors
{\bar{b}_{1}^{0}}
and
{\bar{b}_{3}^{0}}
to “reconstruct”
f_{0}
).
Clearly,
Q
also contains the couples
(\bar{a}_{f_{0}},\;a_{\theta f_{0}})
for all such functions
f_{0}
.

On the other hand, if a couple from
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\times F
is in
T
, then it must be generated by the couples

(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{b},\;b),\;(\bar{c}^{\;2}\!,\;c)
and, hence, its first coordinate has to involve
\bar{c}
in even degrees
only
, e.g., it may never contain subwords like

\bar{b}_{3}=\bar{b}^{\,\bar{c}^{\,3}}
or
\bar{a}^{\,\bar{b}_{5}}
.
But
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
is
freely
generated by elements
\bar{a}_{f}
for
f\!\in\vartheta\mathcal{X}
, such as
f\!=(2,5,3,8)
or
(2,0,3,0)
. Hence, an element
from
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
will be in
\langle\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}^{\;2}\rangle
only if all the coordinates for odd indices in
f
are zero, i.e.,
f=f_{0}
.

The equality
Q=\big\langle(\bar{a}_{f_{0}},\;a_{\theta f_{0}})\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle=\big\langle(\bar{a}_{f_{0}},\;a_{\theta f})\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle

has been proved, and for any
f\in\mathcal{X}
we have the element
a_{\theta f}\in A_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
standing as the
second
coordinate in one of the couples
(\bar{a}_{f_{0}},\;a_{\theta f})
above. This does
not
mean that
\bar{a}_{f}
also occurs as a
first
coordinate in one of those couples, but we are not in need of that fact either.

Since
\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
\bar{F}
for the earlier mentioned
K_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
and
L_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}
, then the direct product

\bar{A}_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\!\times F
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for
K_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\!\times\!F
and
L_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\!\times\!F
.
The subgroup
T=\big\langle(\bar{a},\;a),\;(\bar{b},\;b),\;(\bar{c}^{\;2}\!,\;c)\big\rangle

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
by Remark
2.1
for
K_{T}=\bar{F}\!\times\!F
and
L_{T}=T
.
Hence, by Corollary
5.7
(
1
) their intersection

Q
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented:

K_{Q}=\big((K_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\!\times\!F)*_{L_{\vartheta\mathcal{X}}\times\,F}\;v_{5}\big)*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)*_{T}v_{6}\big)

and for its finitely generated subgroup

L_{Q}=(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{\,v_{5}v_{6}}
.

6.9.4.
“Extracting”
A_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
from
Q

To “extract” the
A_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
from
Q
notice that by Corollary
5.7
(
2
) the join

Q_{1}=\big\langle\bar{F}\!\times\!\{1\},\;Q\big\rangle
is benign in

\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented:

K_{Q_{1}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)*_{\bar{F}\times\{1\}}w_{1}\big)*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big(K_{Q}*_{L_{Q}}w_{2}\big)

and for its finitely generated subgroup:

L_{Q_{1}}=\big\langle(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{1}}\!,\;(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{2}}\big\rangle.

Then by Corollary
5.7
(
1
) the intersection

A_{\theta\mathcal{X}}\!=\big(\{1\}\!\times\!F\big)\cap Q_{1}

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented:

K_{\theta\mathcal{X}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\{1\}\times F}w_{3}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big(K_{Q_{1}}*_{L_{Q_{1}}}\!w_{4}\big)

and for the finitely presented

L_{\theta\mathcal{X}}=(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{3}w_{4}}
.
Since
A_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
is inside
F
, it is benign in
F
also, with the same choice for
K_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
,

L_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
.

6.9.5.
Writing
K_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
explicitly

Now taking into account the above notation we can write:

(6.24)

\begin{split}K_{\theta\mathcal{X}}=\Big\langle X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}};\;\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\};\;y;\;a,b,c;\;v_{1},\ldots,v_{6};w_{1},\ldots,w_{4}\;\mathrel{|}\;R_{\bar{\mathscr{A}}};\;\bar{S};\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$y$ sends $\bar{d},\bar{e}$ \,to\, $\bar{d}_{2},\bar{e}$};\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes the generators of $\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}$};\;\;\;\text{$v_{2}$ fixes $\bar{d}_{1},\,y$};\;\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$v_{3}$ fixes $X_{\!\mathcal{K}}^{v_{1}}\!\cup X_{\!\mathcal{K}}^{v_{2}}$;}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{$v_{4},w_{1}$ fix $\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}$};\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$a,b,c$ commute with $X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}};\;\bar{Z};\;y;\;v_{1},\ldots,v_{4}$};\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$v_{5}$ fixes $X_{\!\mathcal{K}}^{v_{3}v_{4}}$};\hskip 8.53581pt\text{$v_{5},w_{3}$ fix $a,b,c$};\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$v_{6}$ fixes $\bar{a}a,\,\bar{b}b,\,\bar{c}^{\;2}c$};\hskip 8.53581pt\text{$w_{2}$ fixes $\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!v_{5}v_{6}}$};\\
&\hskip-264.61063pt\text{$w_{4}$ fixes $\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{w_{1}}\!\cup\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{w_{2}}$}\Big\rangle\,.\end{split}

By Point
6.4.1
we exclude
a,b,c
from the generators
Z
.
As a finitely generated subgroup in
K_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
take
L_{\theta\mathcal{X}}=(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{3}w_{4}}=\big\langle a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\rangle^{w_{3}w_{4}}
.
If
K_{\mathcal{X}}
has
m
generators and
n
defining relations, and if

L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
has
k
generators, then the group
K_{\theta\mathcal{X}}
in (
6.24
)
has

9+(m-3)+1+3+6+4=m+20
generators and

20+n+2+k+2+2\cdot(m+10)+2\cdot 3+3\cdot(9+m-3+1+4)+(m+10)+2\cdot 3+3+6+2\cdot 6=n+6m+k+111
defining relations.

6.10.
The proof for the operation
\tau

Let
\mathcal{Y}=\tau\mathcal{X}
, i.e., when
\mathcal{X}
contains, say,
f\!=(2,5,3)
, then
\mathcal{Y}
contains
\tau f\!=(5,2,3)
.
Assume the hypothesis of Theorem
A
holds for
\mathcal{X}
: the subgroup
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in
F
for an explicitly given finitely presented
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
and for its finitely generated subgroup
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
.

6.10.1.
Writing
\langle d_{i}\;|\;i\in{\mdmathbb Z}\rangle
as a product of three benign factors

Following the construction in Point
6.8.1
,
reuse the isomorphisms

{}_{m},{}_{m}^{\prime}

on
\langle d,e\rangle
.
For
m=0
we have
{}_{0}(d)=d_{1},\;{}_{0}^{\prime}(d)=d_{0}=d
,

{}_{0}(e)={}_{0}^{\prime}(e)=e^{2}
, using which we can define

\mathscr{B}_{0}=\mathscr{A}*_{{}_{0},{}_{0}^{\prime}}(x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0})
.
Inside this group we clearly have:

\langle d,e,x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}\rangle=\langle d,e\rangle*_{{}_{0},{}_{0}^{\prime}}(x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0})\;\cong\;\Xi_{\,0}.

Using an analog of Lemma
5.10
we for the subgroup
D_{0}=\langle d_{\,-1},d_{\,-2},\ldots\,\rangle
have:

(6.25)

\langle d,e\rangle\cap\langle d_{\,-1},x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}\rangle=D_{0}\quad\text{and also}\quad\mathscr{A}\cap\langle d_{\,-1},x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}\rangle=D_{0},

from where
D_{0}
is benign in
\mathscr{A}
for the finitely presented overgroup

K_{D_{0}}=\mathscr{B}_{0}
and for its finitely generated subgroup

L_{D_{0}}=\langle d_{\,-1},x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}\rangle
.

Similarly, for
m=2
we have
{}_{2}(d)=d_{\,-1},\;{}_{2}^{\prime}(d)=d_{\,-2}
,

{}_{2}(e)={}_{2}^{\prime}(e)=e^{2}
by which we can define

\mathscr{B}_{2}=\mathscr{A}*_{{}_{2},{}_{2}^{\prime}}(x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2})
to discover inside it:

\langle d,e,x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle=\langle d,e\rangle*_{{}_{2},{}_{2}^{\prime}}(x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2})\;\cong\;\Xi_{2}.

Then we for the subgroup
D_{2}=\langle d_{2},d_{3},\ldots\,\rangle
have:

(6.26)

\langle d,e\rangle\cap\langle d_{2},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle=D_{2}\quad\text{and also}\quad\mathscr{A}\cap\langle d_{2},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle=D_{2},

that is,
D_{2}
is benign in
\mathscr{A}
for

K_{D_{2}}=\mathscr{B}_{2}
and for

L_{D_{2}}=\langle d_{2},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle
.

Notice that neither
D_{0}
nor
D_{2}
involved the elements
d_{0}
and
d_{1}
. The subgroup
D_{1}=\langle d_{0},d_{1}\rangle
they generate is benign in
\mathscr{A}
for

K_{D_{1}}=\mathscr{A}
and for

L_{D_{1}}=D_{1}
by Remark
2.1
.

The mentioned three finitely presented groups

K_{D_{0}},K_{D_{1}},K_{D_{2}}
intersect strictly in
\mathscr{A}
, and so we can form the
✻
-construction:

(6.27)

\begin{split}\mathscr{B}&=(K_{D_{0}}*_{L_{D_{0}}}y_{0})\,*_{\!\mathscr{A}}(K_{D_{1}}*_{L_{D_{1}}}y_{1})\,*_{\!\mathscr{A}}(K_{D_{2}}*_{L_{D_{2}}}y_{2})\\
&=(\mathscr{B}_{0}*_{\langle d_{\,-1},x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}\rangle}y_{0})\,*_{\!\mathscr{A}}(\mathscr{A}*_{\langle d_{0},\;d_{1}\rangle}y_{1})\,*_{\!\mathscr{A}}(\mathscr{B}_{2}*_{\langle d_{2},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}\rangle}y_{2})\,.\end{split}

Inside
\langle d,e\rangle

the subgroups

D_{0},D_{1},D_{2}
generate their
free
product:

(6.28)

D_{0}*D_{1}*\,D_{2}=\langle\ldots d_{\,-2},d_{\,-1}\rangle*\langle d_{0},\;d_{1}\rangle*\langle d_{2},d_{3},\ldots\rangle

which is just the free group
\langle d_{i}\;|\;i\in{\mdmathbb Z}\rangle
of countable rank.

6.10.2.
Obtaining the benign subgroup
Q
for
\tau

Since the above subgroups
D_{0},D_{1},D_{2}
also are inside
\mathscr{B}
, then by Corollary
5.9

the subgroups:

\mathscr{A}^{y_{0}}
​,

\mathscr{A}^{y_{1}}
​,

\mathscr{A}^{y_{2}}

together generate in

\mathscr{B}

their
free
product

\mathscr{A}^{y_{0}}*\mathscr{A}^{y_{1}}*\mathscr{A}^{y_{2}}
.
Hence any three isomorphisms on the above free factors
\mathscr{A}^{y_{0}}
​,

\mathscr{A}^{y_{1}}
​,

\mathscr{A}^{y_{2}}
(or on arbitrary subgroups inside them) have a common continuation.
On

\mathscr{A}^{y_{0}}
and
\mathscr{A}^{y_{2}}
choose the identity isomorphisms,
and on the subgroup
D_{1}^{y_{1}}=\langle d_{0}^{y_{1}}\!,\,d_{1}^{y_{1}}\rangle
of the factor
\mathscr{A}^{y_{1}}

choose the swapping isomorphism sending

d_{0}^{y_{1}}\!,\,d_{1}^{y_{1}}
to

d_{1}^{y_{1}}\!,\,d_{0}^{y_{1}}
respectively.
Denote their common continuation on
\mathscr{A}^{y_{0}}*D_{1}^{y_{1}}*\mathscr{A}^{y_{2}}
by
\gamma
.
Clearly,
\gamma
can be well defined by its values on
9+2+9=20
generators in
X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{y_{0}}\cup\big\{d_{0}^{y_{1}}\!,\,d_{1}^{y_{1}}\big\}\cup X_{\!\mathscr{A}}^{y_{2}}
.

As a generating set for
\mathscr{B}
one may choose:

(6.29)

X_{\!\mathscr{B}}=X_{\!\mathscr{A}}\cup\big\{x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2},\,y_{0},y_{1},y_{2}\big\}.

Then in analogy with
Point
6.4.2

we can choose its copy:

(6.30)

X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}}=X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{A}}}\cup\big\{\bar{x}_{0},\bar{x}^{\prime}_{0},\bar{x}_{2},\bar{x}^{\prime}_{2},\,\bar{y}_{0},\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{2}\big\},

and using it build the copy

\bar{\mathscr{B}}
of

{\mathscr{B}}
via a procedure similar to that above.
Inside the direct product
\bar{\mathscr{B}}\!\times\!\mathscr{B}
choose the set of
20
couples:

(6.31)

\Big\{\big(\bar{x}^{\bar{y}_{0}},x^{y_{0}}\big)\mathrel{\;|\,}x\!\in X_{\!{\mathscr{A}}}\Big\}\cup\big\{(\bar{d}_{0}^{\bar{y}_{1}},d_{1}^{y_{1}}),\;(\bar{d}_{1}^{\bar{y}_{1}},d_{0}^{y_{1}})\big\}\cup\Big\{\big(\bar{x}^{\bar{y}_{2}},x^{y_{2}}\big)\mathrel{\;|\,}x\!\in X_{\!{\mathscr{A}}}\Big\}.

Correlation of this set with the above function
\gamma
is easy to notice:
since
\gamma
is an identical map over
\mathscr{A}^{y_{0}}
, then the couples
(\bar{x}^{\bar{y}_{0}},x^{y_{0}})
with
x\!\in X_{\!{\mathscr{A}}}
are the couples

\big(\bar{a}^{\bar{y}_{0}},\gamma(a^{y_{0}})\big)
,
\big(\bar{b}^{\bar{y}_{0}},\gamma(b^{y_{0}})\big),\ldots,\big(\bar{e}^{\bar{y}_{0}},\gamma(e^{y_{0}})\big)
. Similarly for
\mathscr{A}^{y_{2}}
we have the couples
\big(\bar{a}^{\bar{y}_{2}},\gamma(a^{y_{2}})\big)
,
\big(\bar{b}^{\bar{y}_{2}},

\gamma(b^{y_{2}})\big)
,
\ldots,\big(\bar{e}^{\bar{y}_{2}},\gamma(e^{y_{2}})\big)
.
Lastly, since
\gamma
just swaps
d_{0}^{y_{1}}
and
d_{1}^{y_{1}}
, the central two couples in (
6.31
) are equal to

\big(\bar{d}_{0}^{\bar{y}_{1}},\gamma(d_{0}^{y_{1}})\big)
,

\big(\bar{d}_{1}^{\bar{y}_{1}},\gamma(d_{1}^{y_{1}})\big)
.
That is, in (
6.31
) the second coordinate of each couple is the image of the first coordinate under
\gamma
, with just “the bar removed”.

Recall that
\mathscr{B}_{0}
and
\mathscr{B}_{2}
were constructed so that (
6.25
) and (
6.26
) hold. Hence for any of

d_{\,-1},d_{\,-2},\ldots

from
D_{0}

we have
d_{i}^{y_{0}}=d_{\,i}
; and for any of

d_{\,2},d_{3},\ldots

from
D_{2}

we have
d_{i}^{y_{2}}=d_{\,i}
.
Similarly,
d_{0}^{y_{1}}=d_{0}
and
d_{1}^{y_{1}}=d_{1}
, since

L_{D_{1}}=\langle d_{0},d_{1}\rangle
.
Thus, the subgroup
T
generated by
20
couples
(
6.31
) contains the set of all the
infinitely many
couples:

(6.32)

\big\{(\bar{a},\;a)\big\}\cup\big\{(\bar{d}_{i},d_{i})\mathrel{\;|\;}i\in{\mdmathbb Z}\backslash\{0,1\}\big\}\cup\big\{(\bar{d}_{0},d_{1}),\;(\bar{d}_{1},d_{0})\big\}

which is nothing but
\big\{\big(\bar{a},\;\gamma(a)\big)\big\}\cup\big\{\big(\bar{d}_{i},\gamma(d_{i})\big)\mathrel{\;|\;}i\in{\mdmathbb Z}\big\}
.

Now we are ready to again adapt the idea from Point
6.5.1
for the operation
\tau
, i.e., to denote
P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times\langle a,d,e\rangle

in
\bar{F}\!\times F
, and set

Q=T\cap P
to establish that this intersection has the simple structure

Q=\big\langle(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{\tau f}\!)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle
.

T
contains
Q
, for, from (
6.32
) we can deduce that
T
for every
f\!\!\in\mathcal{E}
contains a specific element

\lambda_{f}=\big(\bar{d}_{f},\;\tilde{d}_{f}\big)
,
where
\tilde{d}_{f}
is obtained from
d_{f}
by replacing its two factors

d_{0}^{f(0)}
​​,
d_{1}^{f(1)}
by the factors
d_{1}^{f(0)}
​​,
d_{0}^{f(1)}
respectively.
Say, for
f=(2,5,3)
we have

d_{f}=d_{0}^{2}d_{1}^{5}d_{2}^{3}

and

\tilde{d}_{f}=d_{1}^{2}d_{0}^{5}d_{2}^{3}
.
Then
\lambda_{f}
indeed is in
T
because the couples

(\bar{d}_{0},\;d_{1})
,

(\bar{d}_{1},\;d_{0})
,

(\bar{d}_{2},\;d_{2})

are in
T
, and so the product:

(\bar{d}_{0},\;d_{1})^{2}(\bar{d}_{1},\;d_{0})^{5}(\bar{d}_{2},\;d_{2})^{3}=(\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\bar{d}_{2}^{3},\;d_{1}^{2}d_{0}^{5}d_{2}^{3})=(\bar{d}_{f},\;\tilde{d}_{f})=\lambda_{f}

also is in
T
.
Notice that

\tilde{d}_{f}=d_{1}^{2}d_{0}^{5}d_{2}^{3}

differs from

d_{\tau f}=d_{(5,2,3)}=d_{0}^{5}d_{1}^{2}d_{2}^{3}

in the
order
of factors
d_{0}
and
d_{1}
only.
But since by Remark
5.13
the order of
d_{i}
does not matter in action of
d_{i}
on
a_{f}
, we get
a^{d_{1}^{2}d_{0}^{5}d_{2}^{3}}=a^{d_{0}^{5}d_{1}^{2}d_{2}^{3}}=a^{b_{0}^{5}b_{1}^{2}b_{2}^{3}}=a_{(5,2,3)}=a_{\tau f}
, and so

(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{f})^{\lambda_{f}}=(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{\tau f})

is in
T
.
Since also
Q\subseteq P
, we thus have
Q\subseteq T\cap P
.

To get the reverse inclusion notice that any couple from
P
has its first coordinate inside

\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}
, i.e., that coordinate is generated by some elements
\bar{a}_{f}=\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}=\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}
.
On the other hand, our couple also is in
T
, and from (
6.32
) it follows that if its first coordinate is rewritten as a word on

\bar{a},\bar{d}_{i}
, then the second coordinate in the same couple can be obtained by replacing

\bar{a},\bar{d}_{0},\bar{d}_{1}
by
a,d_{1},d_{0}
, and then
\bar{d}_{i}

by
d_{i}
for all
i\neq 0,1
.
But this just transforms
\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}
to
a^{\tilde{d}_{f}}=a^{b_{\tau f}}=a_{\tau f}
. Say, for
f=(2,5,3)
the first coordinate is:

\bar{a}_{f}=\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}=\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{f}}=\bar{a}^{\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\bar{d}_{2}^{3}}=\bar{d}_{2}^{\;-3}\bar{d}_{1}^{\;-5}\bar{d}_{0}^{\;-2}\,\cdot\,\bar{a}\,\cdot\,\bar{d}_{0}^{2}\,\bar{d}_{1}^{5}\,\bar{d}_{2}^{3}

and then the second coordinate has to be:

d_{2}^{\;-3}d_{0}^{\;-5}d_{1}^{\;-2}\,\cdot\,a\,\cdot\,d_{1}^{2}\,d_{0}^{5}\,d_{2}^{3}=a^{d_{1}^{2}\,d_{0}^{5}\,d_{2}^{3}}=a^{b_{\tau f}}=a_{\tau f}.

Thus,
Q
has a simple description:

Q=\big\langle\big(\bar{a}^{\bar{b}_{f}}\!,\;a^{b_{\tau f}}\!\big)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle=\big\langle(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{\tau f}\!)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle.

Compare the above used elements
\tilde{d}_{f}
and
\lambda_{f}
to their similarly denoted, but yet slightly different, analogs in Point
6.5.1
.

Slightly adapting the construction of Point
6.4.3
notice that
K_{\!\mathcal{X}}
could be built to intersect with
\mathscr{B}
in
F
strictly. This allows us to define

\mathcal{K}=K_{\mathcal{X}}*_{F}\mathscr{B}

(compare this with the group
\mathcal{K}
in Point
6.4.3
), such that

\mathscr{B}\cap L_{\mathcal{X}}=A_{\mathcal{X}}
, that is,

A_{\mathcal{X}}
also is benign in
\mathscr{B}
for the finitely presented overgroup
\mathcal{K}
and for the same finitely generated subgroup
L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
mentioned above.
The analog
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
of
\mathcal{K}
can be constructed for
\bar{\mathscr{B}}
so that

P=\bar{A}_{\mathcal{X}}\times\langle a,d,e\rangle
is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{B}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{B}}
for

K_{P}=\bar{\mathcal{K}}\times\mathscr{B}
and for
L_{P}=\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}\!\times\langle a,d,e\rangle\leq K_{P}
.

The
20
-generator group
T

is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{B}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{B}}
for
K_{T}=\bar{\mathscr{B}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{B}}
and for
L_{T}=T
.
Hence, the intersection
Q
also is benign in
\bar{\mathscr{B}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{B}}
for the finitely presented overgroup:

(6.33)

\begin{split}K_{Q}&=\big(K_{T}*_{L_{T}}v_{1}\big)*_{\bar{\mathscr{B}}\times{\mathscr{B}}}\big(K_{P}*_{L_{P}}v_{2}\big)\\
&=\Big(\big(\bar{\mathscr{B}}\times{\mathscr{B}}\big)*_{T}v_{1}\Big)\,*_{\bar{\mathscr{B}}\times{\mathscr{B}}}\Big(\big(\bar{\mathcal{K}}\!\times\!\mathscr{B}\big)*_{\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}\times\,\langle a,d,e\rangle}v_{2}\Big)\end{split}

and for its
32
-generator subgroup
L_{Q}\!=\big(\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}\!\times\!{\mathscr{B}}\big)^{v_{1}v_{2}}
​.

But since
Q=\big\langle(\bar{a}_{f},\;a_{\tau f}\!)\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle
lies inside
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
, then
Q
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
also for the same choice of
K_{Q}
and
L_{Q}
.

6.10.3.
“Extracting”
A_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
from
Q

In analogy with Point
6.5.2
we “extract” the benign subgroup

A_{\tau\mathcal{X}}=\big\langle a_{\tau f}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\big\rangle
from
Q
, skipping some explanation details below.

The join

Q_{1}=\big\langle\bar{F}\!\times\!\{1\},\,Q\big\rangle=\bar{F}\!\times\!\langle a_{\tau f}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle
is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented:

K_{Q_{1}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\bar{F}\times\{1\}}w_{1}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}\big(K_{Q}*_{L_{Q}}w_{2}\big)

and for its
12
-generator subgroup:

L_{Q_{1}}=\big\langle(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{1}}\!,\;(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{2}}\big\rangle.

The intersection:

A_{\tau\mathcal{X}}=A_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big(\{1\}\!\times\!F\big)\cap Q_{1}=\langle a_{\tau f}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle

is benign in
\bar{F}\!\times\!F
for the finitely presented overgroup:

K_{\tau\mathcal{X}}=K_{\mathcal{Y}}=\big((\bar{F}\!\times\!F)\,*_{\{1\}\times F}w_{3}\big)\,*_{\bar{F}\times F}(K_{Q_{1}}*_{L_{Q_{1}}}w_{4})

and for its
6
-generator subgroup

L_{\tau\mathcal{X}}=L_{\mathcal{Y}}=(\bar{F}\!\times\!F)^{w_{3}w_{4}}
.
But since
A_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
is inside
F
, it is benign in
F
also, for the same choice of
K_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
and
L_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
made above.

6.10.4.
Writing
K_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
explicitly

Recalling the above constructions, in particular, the generating sets

X_{\!\mathscr{B}}
and
X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}}

in
(
6.29
) and
(
6.30
)
we explicitly have:

(6.34)

\begin{split}K_{\tau\mathcal{X}}=\Big\langle X_{\!\mathscr{B}},\;X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}};\;\;\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\};\;y_{0},y_{1},y_{2};\;v_{1},v_{2};\;\;w_{1},\ldots,w_{4}\;\mathrel{|}\\
&\hskip-247.53897ptR_{\!\mathscr{A}};\;R_{\!\mathscr{\bar{A}}};\;\bar{S};\;\\
&\hskip-247.53897ptd^{x_{0}}=d_{\,1},\;d^{x^{\prime}_{0}}={d},\;\;e^{x_{0}}=e^{x^{\prime}_{0}}=e^{2};\;\\
&\hskip-247.53897ptd^{x_{2}}=d_{\,-1},\;d^{x^{\prime}_{2}}={d_{\,-2}},\;\;e^{x_{2}}=e^{x^{\prime}_{2}}=e^{2};\;\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\text{$y_{0}$ fixes $d_{\,-1},x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}$;\,
$y_{1}$ fixes $d_{0},d_{1}$;\,
$y_{2}$ fixes $d_{2},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}$};\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\bar{d}^{\bar{x}_{0}}=\bar{d}_{\,1},\;\bar{d}^{\bar{x}^{\prime}_{0}}={\bar{d}},\;\;\bar{e}^{\bar{x}_{0}}=\bar{e}^{\bar{x}^{\prime}_{0}}=\bar{e}^{\;2};\;\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\bar{d}^{\bar{x}_{2}}=\bar{d}_{\,-1},\;\bar{d}^{\bar{x}^{\prime}_{2}}={\bar{d}_{\,-2}},\;\;\bar{e}^{\bar{x}_{2}}=\bar{e}^{\bar{x}^{\prime}_{2}}=\bar{e}^{\;2};\;\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\text{$y_{0}$ fixes $d_{\,-1},x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0}$;\,
$y_{1}$ fixes $d_{0},d_{1}$;\,
$y_{2}$ fixes $d_{2},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{2}$};\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\text{$X_{\!\mathscr{B}}$ commutes with $X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}}$ and $\bar{Z}\backslash\{\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\}$};\;\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\text{$v_{1}$ fixes $20$ couples \eqref{EQ Couples in BxB};\;
$v_{2}$ fixes
$\bar{L}_{\!\mathcal{X}}$ and $a,d,e$;
}\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\text{$w_{1}$ fixes $\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}$;\; $w_{2}$ fixes
$X_{\!\mathscr{B}}^{\!v_{1}v_{2}}\cup X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}}^{\!v_{1}v_{2}}$};\;\;\text{$w_{3}$ fixes $a,b,c$};\\
&\hskip-247.53897pt\text{$w_{4}$ fixes
$\big\{a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\}^{\!w_{1}}$}\Big\rangle\,.\end{split}

As the finitely generated subgroup
L_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
in
K_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
we can explicitly take

\big\langle a,b,c,\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}\big\rangle^{w_{3}w_{4}}
​​.
In (
6.34
) the notation

X_{\!\mathscr{B}}^{\!v_{1}v_{2}}

stands for the set of conjugates of all generators from
X_{\!\mathscr{B}}
by
v_{1}v_{2}
; and

X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}}^{\!v_{1}v_{2}}
is defined analogously.
In the
1
’st and
9
’th lines of (
6.34
) we exclude
\bar{a},\bar{b},\bar{c}
from
\bar{Z}
because they were already included in
X_{\!\bar{\mathscr{B}}}
.
If
K_{\mathcal{X}}
has
m
generators and
n
defining relations, and if

L_{\!\mathcal{X}}
has
k
generators, then the group
K_{\tau\mathcal{X}}
in (
6.34
)
has

16+16+(m-3)+3+2+4=m+38
generators and

20+20+n+2\cdot(4+4+3+2+3)+16\cdot(16+m-3)+20+(k+3)+3+2\cdot 16+3+2\cdot 6=n+16m+k+345
defining relations.

6.11.
The proof for the operation
\omega_{m}

Assume the hypothesis of Theorem
A
holds for
\mathcal{X}
,
the group
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle
with its subgroup
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
are given explicitly, and denote
\mathcal{Y}=\omega_{m}\mathcal{X}
for some
m=1,2,\ldots
,
see a simple example for
m=3
in (
6.39
).

Two agreements are going to simplify the proofs below.
Firstly, since the names of free generators do not actually matter, later we are going to suppose that
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in a free group of rank
3
on some differently named generators
g,h,k
which will be introduced below, compare with Section
2.4
.
Secondly, for the set
\mathcal{X}_{m}\!=\!\mathcal{X}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
it is trivial to notice that
\omega_{m}(\mathcal{X})=\omega_{m}(\mathcal{X}_{m})
, and so without loss of generality we may reduce our consideration to the case
\mathcal{X}\subseteq{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
.
This, in particular, allows us to write all
f
in
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{X}_{m}
as sequences
f=(j_{0},\ldots,j_{m-1})
. If a
shorter
sequence contains less than
m
integers, we can without loss of generality extend its length to
m
by appending some extra
0
’s at the end, see Section
2.2
.

6.11.1.
The groups
\Gamma
and
\mathscr{G}

In the groups
\Xi_{m}
and
\Xi_{0}
we by Lemma
5.10
have:

\begin{split}\langle b,c\rangle\cap\langle b_{m},t_{m},t^{\prime}_{m}\rangle=\langle b_{m},b_{m+1},\ldots\rangle&\text{ in }\Xi_{m},\\
\langle b,c\rangle\cap\langle b_{-1},t_{0},t^{\prime}_{0}\rangle=\langle b_{-1},b_{-2},\ldots\rangle&\text{ in }\Xi_{0}.\\
\end{split}

In analogy with the group
\mathscr{C}
given in (
5.8
),
build the finitely presented
✻
-construction:

\mathscr{Z}=\left(\Xi_{m}*_{\langle b_{m},t_{m},t^{\prime}_{m}\rangle}r_{1}\right)\,*_{\langle b,c\rangle}\left(\Xi_{0}*_{\langle b_{-1},t_{0},t^{\prime}_{0}\rangle}r_{2}\right).

In
\langle b,c\rangle
the subgroup

B_{m}=\langle\ldots b_{-2},b_{-1};\;\;b_{m},b_{m+1},\ldots\rangle
is the join of the above
\langle b_{m},b_{m+1},\ldots\rangle
and
\langle b_{-1},b_{-2},\ldots\rangle
, and so by Corollary
5.7
it is benign in
\langle b,c\rangle
. As a finitely presented overgroup of
\langle b,c\rangle
one can take
K_{B_{m}}\!=\mathscr{Z}
, and as its finitely generated subgroup one can pick
L_{B_{m}}\!=P_{m}\!=\big\langle\langle b,c\rangle^{r_{1}}\!,\,\langle b,c\rangle^{r_{2}}\big\rangle
, see Corollary
5.7
(
2
). Also, check Figure 8 in

[
32
]
illustrating this construction.

The letters
g,h,k
were not so far used, and we may now involve them to build:

(6.35)

\Gamma=\langle b,c\rangle*_{B_{m}}\!(g,h,k)\quad{\rm and}\quad\mathscr{G}=\mathscr{Z}*_{P_{m}}\!(g,h,k)

with three stable letters
g,h,k
all fixing the subgroups
B_{m}
and
P_{m}
​ respectively.
The second one of the groups (
6.35
) clearly is finitely presented, because
\mathscr{Z}
is finitely presented, and
P_{m}
is finitely generated.
Taking into account
\langle b,c\rangle\cap\,P_{m}\!=B_{m}
,
and using
P_{m}
​ and
B_{m}
as the groups
A
and
A^{\prime}

of Corollary 3.5 (1), and Remark 3.6
from

[
32
]
, we see that
\Gamma
is a subgroup of
\mathscr{G}
, given as an intersection:

(6.36)

\langle b,c,g,h,k\rangle=\langle b,c\rangle*_{\langle b,c\rangle\;\cap\;P_{m}}\!(g,h,k)=\Gamma.

For this group, in analogy with the elements
b_{i},b_{f}
and
a_{f}
, define the elements

h_{i}=h^{k^{i}}
​​,

h_{f}=\cdots h_{-1}^{f(-1)}h_{0}^{f(0)}h_{1}^{f(1)}\cdots
,
and

g_{f}=g^{h_{f}}
​, see Section
2.4
.

Since the particular names of free generators of
F
do not actually matter, we may suppose the analog
G_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle g^{h_{f}}\;|\;f\in\mathcal{X}\rangle
of
A_{\mathcal{X}}
is benign in the free group
F^{\prime}=\langle g,h,k\rangle
of rank
3
, and the respective finitely presented overgroup
K_{\mathcal{X}}=\langle\,Z\;|\;S\rangle

of

F^{\prime}
,
and the finitely generated
L_{\mathcal{X}}\leq K_{\mathcal{X}}
, with
F^{\prime}\cap L_{\mathcal{X}}=G_{\mathcal{X}}
, are explicitly given.
In the beginning of Section
6.11
we introduced
K_{\mathcal{X}},L_{\mathcal{X}}
for
F
, but using the same symbols for
F^{\prime}
should cause no confusion.

6.11.2.
Construction of
\Delta

The free group
\langle b,c\rangle
contains a free subgroup
\langle b_{i}\mathrel{|}i\in{\mdmathbb Z}\rangle
of countable rank, which is a free product
B_{m}*\,\tilde{B}_{m}
with

B_{m}
mentioned above, and with
its
m
-generator “complement”

\tilde{B}_{m}\!=\!\langle b_{0},\ldots b_{m-1}\rangle
.
In
\Gamma
pick the subgroup
R=\langle g_{f}b_{f}^{-1}\mathrel{|}f\in\mathcal{E}_{m}\rangle
, and since the letter
a
was
not
